- [ ] **Step 6: Update `web/README.md`**

Add these sections after the existing "Shared build pages and the Worker" section:

````markdown
## Combat log reports

A report's files are written to the R2 bucket `foreversixty-logs` by the API and served to
the browser by this site's own Worker at `/logs-data/reports/<id>/…` — same origin, same
edge cache, no CORS, and no origin call on a repeat view of a closed fight. The keys are
the engine's `store.Keys` exactly:

```
reports/<id>/report.json              metadata, health, fight list, units   (max-age=5)
reports/<id>/fights/<n>/summary.json  the precomputed tables                (immutable)
reports/<id>/fights/<n>/events.parquet  typed events, queried in the browser  (immutable)
reports/<id>/fights/<n>/live.json     the snapshot while a fight is open     (max-age=5)
```

R2 does not know a report is private, so `src/worker.ts` is the enforcement point: it asks
the API for the report's visibility, caches the answer for sixty seconds, serves public and
unlisted, and refuses private and guild with 403. Those two carry a signed `data_base_url`
from `GET /v1/reports/{id}/access` instead.

### The shells

`/reports/<id>`, `/rankings/<slug>`, `/character/<region>/<ruleset>/<name>` and
`/guild/<region>/<ruleset>/<name>` do not exist at build time. The Worker serves one static
shell per prefix — `dist/reports.html` and its three siblings — and rewrites the `<title>`,
canonical and Open Graph tags from the API's JSON with `HTMLRewriter`. The values come from
`src/lib/report/og-meta.ts`, which is pure and unit-tested; `src/test-support/html-rewriter.ts`
is a small stand-in for the runtime global, because `HTMLRewriter` has no Node
implementation and `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` peers on vitest 4 while this project is
on 5.

### The report island

`dist/report-island.js` is a second standalone bundle, built exactly like the planner's by
`vite.report-island.config.ts`, and budgeted at 140 KB gzipped by
`scripts/check-island-size.mjs`. Everything it computes lives in plain TypeScript under
`src/lib/report/`:

| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `types.ts` | TypeScript mirrors of the engine's JSON, field for field |
| `url.ts` | the page's whole state, which is its query string |
| `load.ts` | the API and `data_base_url`, plus the five-second live poll |
| `window.ts` | rescoping every table to a brushed window, from the per-second series |
| `filters.ts` | target, ability, boss-only, players-only, overkill, after-death |
| `percentile.ts` | parse percentiles, six at a time, cached |
| `events.ts` | the Events view's list, built from what the summary timestamps |
| `query.ts` | the DuckDB-WASM query layer, behind an interface the tests fake |
| `planner-link.ts` | a combatant's gear and talents as an FS1 link into the planner |

### Deep queries

The Queries view runs SQL over the fight's own `events.parquet` with DuckDB-WASM. It loads
lazily — never on page load, asserted by an e2e — and from `public/duckdb/`, which
`scripts/sync-duckdb.mjs` fills from `node_modules` at build time. The package's own
jsDelivr helper is deliberately unused: no page on this site makes a third-party request.

Spec section 9 asks for a timing test of a brush over a 10 MB fight. The checked-in fixture's
Parquet is 18 KB, so that budget is measured against a real raid log once one exists rather
than against a fixture standing in for one; `tests/e2e/report-queries.spec.ts` measures a
real DuckDB query over the real fixture in the meantime.

### Running it locally

```bash
npm run dev                      # real data, no fixture report
FOREVER_DATA=fixture npm run dev # adds /reports/fixture2abcd and /logs-data/
npm run make:report-fixture      # regenerates src/fixtures/report/ with the engine CLI
```

The fixture report exists only under `FOREVER_DATA=fixture`: a production build prerenders
no fixture pages and publishes no `public/logs-data/`.

## Accounts, logs and rankings

| Page | Island | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `/login` | `Account` (`login`) | Battle.net, or a single-use email link |
| `/account` | `Account` (`account`) | devices and pairing, characters, the anonymize toggle, sign out |
| `/logs` | `Account` (`pairing`, `reports`), `Upload` | live-logging steps, the companion downloads, whole-file upload, your reports |
| `/rankings/<slug>` | `Rankings` | character and guild boards, every filter in the URL |
| `/character/…`, `/guild/…` | `Character`, `Guild` | ranked history, bests, builds, progression |

The header shows who is signed in only on these pages and on the report shells: `Base.astro`
takes a `session` prop, and a content page that set it would ship client JavaScript for a
link and would not hold its Lighthouse budget.

Sessions are an opaque `HttpOnly` cookie, so no code here reads one — `credentials:
'include'` is the whole mechanism — and every state-changing call sends the readable
`fs_csrf` cookie back in `X-CSRF-Token`.
````

## Contract ambiguities resolved

Collected here so the api and companion plans can see every place this plan read the contract rather than quoting it. Each one is also explained where it is implemented.

The contract's **Amendments** section (Sept 14, after the three plans were cross-checked) is authoritative and settled rows 1, 2 and 3 below in this plan's favour, with the page size, the pairing field and the upload ceiling adjusted to the server. Those three are kept here with their outcome rather than deleted, so a reader of either plan can see what was decided and why. Rows 4 onward are still this plan's own readings.

| # | Contract says | What this plan does, and why |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | First draft: `fight_index` is "0-based" | **Settled in the Amendments:** `fight_index` is 1-based everywhere, the engine's own numbers (`fight.Fight.Index`; verified against `logs/engine/event/testdata/v16.log`, which yields 1, 2, 3). This plan uses them, and `defaultState(firstFight)` takes the first entry of `report.json` rather than assuming a number. (Task 2) |
| 2 | First draft: `/logs` needs a "Your reports" list with no route to answer it | **Settled in the Amendments:** `GET /v1/reports?mine=1&page=<n>` → `{ rows: [{ id, title, zone, status, visibility, created_at, fight_count, kill_count }], total, page, per_page: 100 }`. The row shape is this plan's; the page size is 100, not the 50 first proposed here. (Task 7) |
| 3 | First draft: `/account` needs sign out and an anonymize toggle with no routes named | **Settled in the Amendments:** `DELETE /v1/sessions` (204, clears both cookies) and `PATCH /v1/me { anonymize }` (returns the updated `me`), both session plus CSRF. (Task 6) |
| 4 | OG tags "injected via HTMLRewriter" | Kept literally in the Worker. `HTMLRewriter` has no Node implementation and `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` peers on vitest ^4.1.0 against this project's ^5, so the tests stub the global with `src/test-support/html-rewriter.ts` — an implementation of the exact selector and method subset the Worker uses, tested on its own, which throws on anything outside it. (Task 5) |
| 5 | Worker "learns visibility by fetching `GET /v1/reports/{id}/visibility` (cached 60 s)" | Cached in a module-level `Map` in the Worker isolate rather than `caches.default`: per-isolate is the granularity the contract asks for, it survives across requests as isolates do, and it is testable in plain vitest with fake timers. (Task 4) |
| 6 | The report island is "a separate entry like `planner-island.js`" and mounted `client:load` | Kept as a separate standalone bundle, which is why the shells link `/report-island.js` and `/report-island.css` directly instead of using Astro's `client:load`: an Astro island chunk is content-hashed, which defeats the fixed URL the phrase asks for. The island still mounts at load, from `src/report-island.ts`. (Task 9) |
| 7 | OG image for rankings, character and guild | The contract names a card renderer only for reports (`/reports/{id}/card.png`). Those three shells use the site's own static card, `https://foreversixty.gg/og/index.png`, which is already built. (Task 5) |
| 8 | Spec section 4's "phase presets" | Forever's encounters publish no phase data and the engine records none. The presets are the ones the data supports: the whole fight, the first and last thirty seconds, and the twenty seconds before each death. (Task 10) |
| 9 | Spec section 4's "weighted damage" filter | Weighting needs a per-encounter table Forever has not published. The control is **Boss damage only** and is labelled as what it does, keeping damage aimed at the unit whose name is the fight's name. (Task 11) |
| 10 | Spec section 4's "pets" filter | The engine folds a pet's damage into its owner, so there are no pet rows to toggle. The filter bar says so in one line and the Queries view splits them from `adv_owner_guid`. (Task 11) |
| 11 | Spec section 4's table rescoping "instantly, in the browser, from the fight's events" | The events file is 2 to 10 MB and loading it on every brush would break the "instant" it is meant to serve. Totals, auras, casts, deaths and resources rescope **exactly** from the summary; the per-ability and per-target split, which the engine stores only whole-fight, is scaled and marked with a `~` and one line of copy. The exact split is one click away in Queries. (Task 10) |
| 12 | "Deaths and gear link to the player's build" | The link is `/planner?code=<FS1 string>`, the addon's own export format, so the planner reads it, the addon writes it and a player can paste one from either. No combat-log event records race, so the field is empty and the planner picks the first legal one; the retail roster decoder emits chosen spell ids rather than per-talent ranks, so the link carries gear alone and says so in its own label until the Sept 17 beta log settles Forever's shape. (Tasks 13, 14) |
| 13 | `character_key` is `<region>/<ruleset>/<name-slug>`, and the log writes `Name-Realm` | `src/lib/characters.ts` splits a logged unit name on its last hyphen for display and never treats the trailing segment as a ruleset. Ruleset comes from the API, which is the only thing that knows it. (Task 2) |
| 14 | Signed R2 part urls for whole-file upload | The browser must read the `ETag` off each part response to send it back, which needs `ExposeHeaders: ETag` on the bucket's CORS rule. Recorded in Task 8 as a dependency the api plan owns. |
| 15 | `POST /v1/devices/pair` returns `{ code }` "valid 10 minutes" | **Settled in the Amendments:** the payload is `{ code, expires_in }`, seconds. The pairing copy prints `expires_in / 60` rather than the hard-coded ten, so a change on the server changes the sentence. (Task 6) |
| 16 | Whole-file upload's "size ceiling" is unnumbered in the spec | **Settled in the Amendments:** 4 GiB on both sides. `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` matches it exactly, so the browser refuses the same file the server would. (Task 8) |
| 17 | `GET /v1/rankings/guilds` rows | **Settled in the Amendments:** guild rows carry no `state`. They are aggregates rather than a single ranked kill, so there is nothing for a per-kill moderation state to attach to. (Tasks 17, 19) |
| 18 | `builds_seen` on the character page | **Settled in the Amendments:** `{ talent_split, spec?, first_seen }`, with no `build_id` — a saved planner build hashes the order points were spent in, and the combat log does not record that. The page renders the split as text with one line saying why it is not a link. A ranking row's own optional `build_id` is unaffected and still links into `/b/<id>`. (Task 20) |
| 19 | Character `best[]`/`history[]` and guild `progression[]`/`roster_best[]` row shapes | **Settled in the Amendments:** fight rows carry `encounter_id` and `difficulty` and are the same shape in both lists; `first_kill_at` is absent rather than null until a boss dies; and a roster best nests its player as `{ key, name, class, spec }`, which is what makes the name linkable to `/character/…`. (Task 20) |
| 20 | `GET /v1/rankings?encounter=` | **Settled in the Amendments:** the endpoint accepts either the numeric encounter id or the slug `/rankings/<encounter-slug>` uses, so the slug this plan sends from the path needs no translation. (Tasks 17, 19) |
| 21 | `GET /v1/devices` response | **Confirmed unchanged in the Amendments:** the device array is the envelope's `data` directly, which is what `listDevices` already assumes and what Task 6's fixture returns. |

Task 16's e2e measures a real DuckDB query over the real fixture and Task 21's Lighthouse run covers the island's boot; the 10 MB brush timing needs a real raid log, which section 10 schedules for the first beta log. **Recorded rather than faked:** the implementer of Task 21 adds one line to `web/README.md` under "Deep queries" saying the 10 MB budget is measured against a real log once one exists, and does not invent a fixture to stand in for it. |
| 10, sequencing | This plan is the "report page second" slice, and depends only on the api plan's routes |

**2. Placeholder scan.** Every task carries the code it needs. Two lines of rendered copy deliberately say "arrives in Task N", both in `ReportView.svelte`'s tab ladder and both replaced by the task they name (Task 11's line by Task 12, Task 12's by Task 14). Nothing else in the plan defers work: no task says "add error handling", "write tests for the above", "similar to Task N", or "TBD".

**3. Type consistency.** Checked across tasks:
- `ReportState`, `parseReportState`, `reportSearch`, `withState`, `defaultState` (Task 2) are used with those names in Tasks 9, 10, 11.
- `TimeWindow`, `windowOf`, `scopeSummary`, `isFullWindow`, `deathWindow`, `windowPresets` (Task 10) are used with those names in Tasks 9, 11, 14, 15, 16, 17.
- `ScopedActor.approximate` (Task 10) is the field Task 11's `ActorTable` reads as its `approximate` prop.
- `percentileKey` and `createPercentileLoader` (Task 11) are both imported by `ReportView` in Task 11's step 12.
- `splitUnitName`, `characterHref`, `guildHref`, `rulesetLabel` (Task 2) are used with those names in Tasks 6, 9, 11, 12, 14, 15, 17, 19, 20.
- `encodeFS1`/`decodeFS1` (Task 13) and `plannerLinkFor` (Task 14) agree on `FS1Build`'s field names.
- `fetchRankings`, `fetchCharacter`, `fetchGuild`, `RankingRow`, `CharacterPage`, `GuildPage` (Task 17) are used with those names in Tasks 19 and 20.
- `ReportMeta` gains `phase?: string` in Task 11 and is read as `meta?.phase` there and nowhere else.
- `shell-paths.ts` grows `fixtureReportPaths` (Task 5), `fixtureRankingsPaths` (Task 19), `fixtureCharacterPaths` and `fixtureGuildPaths` (Task 20); each task adds its own and none renames another's.

**4. One thing to watch during execution.** `ReportView.svelte` is touched by nine tasks. Each one says exactly where its code goes, but an implementer who takes Task 15 before Task 12 will find a different `{:else}` branch than the one their task describes. Execute the report tasks in order.

## Execution

Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-phase-3-web.md`. Two execution options:

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** — a fresh subagent per task, reviewed between tasks, fast iteration. Tasks 1 to 5 must run in order and first; Tasks 19 and 20 can run in parallel with Tasks 9 to 18 given a second agent.

**2. Inline Execution** — execute the tasks in this session with `superpowers:executing-plans`, batching with checkpoints.

Which approach?
